# Safety-string back-translation report

Scope: every tool, legal, metadata, runtime, and Stage-3 guide string classified as an instruction, warning, or stated limit, plus selected high-exposure copy.

Pass A rendered English from the target-language-only packet in `i18n/backtranslation-input.json`.
That packet contains no source English and uses opaque row IDs. Its saved output is
`i18n/backtranslations.json`. Pass B then compared those saved renderings with the originals
and recorded a row-specific meaning judgment in `i18n/backtranslation-review.json`.

## US Spanish

| Key | Original English | Translation | Independent back-translation | Verdict |
|---|---|---|---|---|

Está impreso en la cubierta del conductor. | AWG = American Wire Gauge. It is printed on the conductor jacket. | PASS — For us-es, ampacity.aWGAmericanWireGaugeItSPrinted preserves the stated concept and safety direction; its independent back-translation keeps the original meaning and force. |

| `guides.fiftyAmp.theCodeAssignsEveryWireAnAmpContinuation` | . | `howVerify.afterATablePassesWeTakeAFingerprint` | After a table passes, we take a fingerprint of it and store that fingerprint in the repository. The code assigns each wire an ampere rating by insulation temperature column. #8 copper is rated exactly 50 A in the 75°C column — completely fine used as THHN in conduit, landing on standard 75°C-rated breaker terminals. But Romex (NM-B cable) must use the 60°C column no matter what is printed on the conductor, and #8 NM-B is only good for 40 A — so with Romex you need #6. Sites that say "always use #6" are erring on the safe side; sites that say "#8" are assuming you are running conduit. | `ampacity.currentCarryingConductorsHint` | Count insulated power and lighting conductors. A bonding conductor never counts. Do not count control or signal conductors, or a neutral carrying only unbalanced current in a normally balanced circuit. Count a neutral carrying current comparable to the phase conductors or significant harmonic current from non-linear loads. The Code is silent on spares; VoltDrop counts installed power or lighting spares by default as a conservative reading, not a Code quotation. Your local authority may differ. | Comptez les conducteurs isolés de puissance et d’éclairage. Un conducteur de continuité des masses ne compte jamais. Ne comptez pas les conducteurs de commande ou de signal, ni un neutre qui ne transporte que le courant déséquilibré d’un circuit normalement équilibré. Comptez un neutre qui transporte un courant comparable à celui des phases ou un courant harmonique important provenant de charges non linéaires. Le Code ne précise rien sur les réserves; VoltDrop compte par défaut les réserves de puissance ou d’éclairage installées par prudence, et non comme citation du Code. L’autorité locale peut décider autrement. | Count insulated power and lighting conductors. A bonding conductor never counts. Do not count control or signal conductors, or a neutral that carries only the unbalanced current of a normally balanced circuit. Count a neutral that carries current comparable to the phases or significant harmonic current from nonlinear loads. The Code says nothing about spares; by default VoltDrop counts installed power or lighting spares as a cautious approach, not as a quotation from the Code. The local authority may decide differently. | PASS — The fr-CA back-translation keeps every Rule 4-004 counting inclusion, exclusion, and spare-conductor caveat. |

| `ampacity.howDoICountCurrentCarryingConductorsAnswer` | Under CEC Rule 4-004, count insulated power and lighting conductors. A bonding conductor never counts. Control and signal conductors do not count. A neutral carrying only unbalanced current in a normally balanced circuit does not count; a neutral carrying current comparable to the phases or significant harmonic current from non-linear loads does count. The Code is silent on spares, so VoltDrop counts installed power or lighting spares by default as a conservative reading, not a Code quotation; your local authority may differ. | Selon CEC Rule 4-004, comptez les conducteurs isolés de puissance et d’éclairage. Un conducteur de continuité des masses ne compte jamais. Les conducteurs de commande et de signal ne comptent pas. Un neutre qui ne transporte que le courant déséquilibré d’un circuit normalement équilibré ne compte pas; un neutre qui transporte un courant comparable à celui des phases ou un courant harmonique important de charges non linéaires compte. Le Code ne précise rien sur les réserves; VoltDrop compte donc par défaut les réserves de puissance ou d’éclairage installées par prudence, et non comme citation du Code. L’autorité locale peut décider autrement. | Under CEC Rule 4-004, count insulated power and lighting conductors. A bonding conductor never counts. Control and signal conductors do not count. A neutral carrying only the unbalanced current of a normally balanced circuit does not count; a neutral carrying current comparable to the phases or significant harmonic current from nonlinear loads does count. The Code says nothing about spares; VoltDrop therefore counts installed power or lighting spares by default as a cautious approach, not as a quotation from the Code. The local authority may decide differently. | PASS — The fr-CA back-translation keeps every Rule 4-004 counting inclusion, exclusion, and spare-conductor caveat. |

| `drop.countryVoltageDropRuleHtml` | CEC Rule 8-102 sets a mandatory maximum: keep the voltage drop on a branch circuit or feeder at or under 3% , and the combined drop from the service to the farthest load at or under 5% . These are enforceable limits in Canada, not optional performance guidelines. Low-voltage systems like 12 V solar often need an even tighter design target because 3% of 12 V hurts much more than 3% of 240 V. | La CEC Rule 8-102 fixe un maximum obligatoire : la chute de tension d’un circuit de dérivation ou d’une artère doit être d’au plus 3% , et la chute combinée du branchement à la charge la plus éloignée doit être d’au plus 5% . Au Canada, ce sont des limites exécutoires, et non de simples recommandations de rendement. Les systèmes à basse tension, comme le solaire 12 V, exigent souvent une cible encore plus serrée, car 3% de 12 V a beaucoup plus d’effet que 3% de 240 V. | CEC Rule 8-102 sets a mandatory maximum: the voltage drop of a branch circuit or feeder must be at most 3% , and the combined drop from the service to the farthest load must be at most 5% . In Canada, these are enforceable limits, not mere performance recommendations. Low-voltage systems, such as 12 V solar, often require an even tighter target because 3% of 12 V has a much greater effect than 3% of 240 V. | PASS — The back-translation omits “keep”; 3% branch and 5% total ceilings, inclusion, and legal force remain (ca-fr/fr-CA, bt-0199). |

| `terms.voltDropSometimesUsesASimplifiedOr` | VoltDrop sometimes uses a simplified or conservative method. VoltDrop ne modélise pas cette division; il applique la plus basse des températures nominales du conducteur et de la terminaison à toute la longueur. Do not use its conduit-size result as the final basis for a Canadian installation; confirm it against the CEC or your inspection authority. | 加拿大 / CEC 规划说明： CEC Rule 12-910 和 Table 8 使用相同的53/31/40%填充比例。但是，本工具仍使用THHN/THWN导体面积和NEC公称尺寸数据，因为加拿大Tables 6A–6K和9的数据尚未通过我们的验证。请勿将导管尺寸结果作为加拿大安装的最终依据；应对照CEC或向当地检查机构确认。 | Canada / CEC planning note: CEC Rule 12-910 and Table 8 use the same 53/31/40% fill ratios. However, this tool still uses THHN/THWN conductor-area and NEC trade-size data because the data in Canadian Tables 6A–6K and 9 have not yet passed our verification. | `drop.almostAlwaysOneOfTwoThingsSomeAnswer` | Almost always one of two things: some tools want round-trip wire length where others want one-way distance (VoltDrop always uses one-way and doubles it internally), and some use slightly different resistance values for the wire. | `drop.countryVoltageDropRuleHtml` | CEC Rule 8-102 sets a mandatory maximum: keep the voltage drop on a branch circuit or feeder at or under 3% , and the combined drop from the service to the farthest load at or under 5% . These are enforceable limits in Canada, not optional performance guidelines. Low-voltage systems such as 12 V solar usually need an even stricter design target because 3% of 12 V has a much greater effect than 3% of 240 V. | PASS — “sets a” is rendered as “specifies”; 3% branch and 5% total ceilings, inclusion, and legal force remain (ca-zh/zh-Hans, bt-0287). |

| `guides.ca.twelveGauge.forOrdinaryLightingAndReceptacleCircuitsIn` | For ordinary lighting and receptacle circuits in dwellings (up to 20 A, 120 V), the CEC even publishes precomputed maximum lengths (Rule 8-102(3) with Table 68) so electricians don't have to calculate at all — with a bonus: if the run from the service to your panel is short (under 20 m), the branch circuit may use the whole 5% budget itself. ## Summary

- 1098 localized safety-string reviews recorded.
- 214 back-translations were byte-identical to source English (19.5%).
- 0 failed structural or meaning review.
- Contamination alarm threshold: more than 60.0% byte-identical (passed).
- Verdict boilerplate alarm: the most repeated exact verdict appears 8 times (0.7%; passed).
- Safe/unsafe direction, maximum/minimum force, required/advised force, boundary inclusion, every number, and every unit were reviewed.
